Scientists Build Biocomputer That Runs on Bacteria to Solve Hard Problems

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Our gifting experts are obsessively searching for holiday gifts that are unique, thoughtful and suitable for any budget.

Our expert, award-winning staff selects the products we cover and rigorously researches and tests our top picks. If you buy through our links, we may earn a commission.

The study relied on the bacterium E. coli and V. natriegens to run the calculations.

Move over, microchips. The next generation of supercomputing might just run on living bacteria. For 20 years, an international team of scientists has been developing a biocomputer that uses bacteria to process information. Scientists from McGill University in Montreal announced their findings on Nov. 3 and published a paper detailing the results earlier this year in Nature.

Researchers from Canada, Europe, the United Kingdom and Australia contributed to this work, which combines biology with computer science and engineering. The researchers say the resulting biocomputer could be used to solve problems that give standard computers a hard time.

“Electronic (sequential) computers are not meant, or designed, to solve decision trees,” Dan Nicolau, a McGill bioengineering professor and senior author for the research paper, told CNET via email.

Nicolau noted that if electronic computers are to solve these types of problems, the computers will need a lot of RAM.

The study relied on the bacterium Escherichia coli and Vibrio natriegens to run the calculations. The researchers used E. coli because it moves through fluid in a predictable, measurable way and V. natriegens because it reproduces very quickly.

By using live bacteria rather than digital processing, the team says the biocomputer can solve big, complex problems at a small fraction of the memory and resources that regular computers require.

The researchers tested the biocomputer with a seemingly simple math problem called the Subset Sum Problem, which famously becomes exponentially harder as the number of inputs increases.

When the Subset Sum Problem inputs grow too large, the problem is known to give traditional computers issues, such as taking centuries, or even millions of years, to solve the problem.

The researchers translated the problem into a graph that branches off into each possible combination of inputs. Next, the team created a tiny physical structure that mirrored the Subset Sum Problem graph.

Inside the structure, they built a network of tunnels to mirror the graph branches. Each fork in the tunnels represents a decision point, and every possible route through the tunnels represents a different solution to the problem.

The researchers unleashed bacteria, which rapidly reproduce, inside the tunnels. The fast-growing bacteria moved through the tunnels, exploring many routes to the solution simultaneously.

Compared to digital processing, the bacteria are slow. But rather than tackling a problem sequentially, as traditional computers do, the bacteria can examine many solutions at once. And if you need more computing power, bacterial reproduction within the tunnels creates additional processing units to help solve the problem.

The team used automated image analysis to follow the bacteria through the correct tunnels. In this way, the researchers were able to tell which paths through the network satisfied the problem’s constraints.

The researchers were also able to adjust temperatures to prevent bacteria overcrowding while traveling through the network.

Enterprising fans have figured out how to run Doom, the groundbreaking 1993 first-person shooter video game, on everything from E. coli to a thermostat. Nicolau says Doom could probably run on the new system, with an important caveat concerning the biocomputer’s size.

“(The biocomputer) must be huge!” Nicolau said. Because bacteria make slow processors, you would need a massive biocomputer to host enough bacteria to run a video game.

But because no RAM is needed, Nicolau said biocomputing could help to solve the global RAM shortage, and even broaden our understanding of what a computer could be.

“A computer is a device which accepts (input) numbers and gets out (output) numbers. Full stop,” he said. “All other uses of the word computer (are) from our point of view, well, poetical.”

2027 Colors of the Year: The Shades Defining Design

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Each fall, the design world turns its attention to the year ahead: What colors and trends will shape the next 12 months? Leading paint manufacturers offer their predictions through annual Color of the Year selections, drawing on evolving aesthetics, cultural influences, and shifting attitudes toward the spaces we inhabit.

But these selections do more than anticipate what’s next. Over time, Colors of the Year have become influential trendsetters in their own right, inspiring everything from interiors and architecture to furniture, fashion, and product design. And while each brand brings its own perspective to the process, their collective choices often reveal broader shifts in how we think about color.

For 2027, greens, earthy browns, and muted blues feature prominently, reflecting a continued interest in nature, comfort, and spaces that feel personal and lived-in. As this year’s selections are unveiled, we’re taking a closer look at the shades making their mark, the thinking behind each choice, and what they might tell us about the year to come.

Drawing on the raw, natural beauty of materials, Cottage Door by Valspar brings a sense of familiarity to the home. This muted blue reflects the growing shift toward intentional living, highlighting design choices as a form of self-reflection. Not defined by seasons or fleeting trends, Cottage Door seamlessly adapts to the natural world’s changing color palettes. Its understated character embraces the beauty of imperfection, celebrating the memories and lived-in details that personalize a space.

Behr’s 2027 Color of the Year, Grounded, invites the outdoors in. Merging mossy olive hues with brown undertones, the shade evokes a sense of stability. Grounded blurs the line between interiors and the natural world, transforming living spaces into natural sanctuaries.

Celery by Sherwin-Williams introduces a fresh perspective to interiors, creating an organic, balanced environment within a soace. Its airy, pastel hue combines tranquility and vitality, emphasizing the relationship between interior design and well-being. Whether used as a quiet background or prominent accent, Celery brings a restorative quality to interiors, striking a balance between organic neutrals and contemporary color palettes.

Stepping beyond momentary trends, Benjamin Moore’s Webster Green carries the sophistication of classic design. Balancing tradition with contemporary appeal, this elegant green reflects the present while looking toward the future, bringing character to any room.

Artifact, Glidden’s choice for 2027 Color of the Year, offers a fresh perspective on interiors through a calm, muted aqua hue, refreshing the home without the need for a complete renovation. A versatile backdrop for personal expression, Artifact reimagines the minimalist white trend, preserving the simplicity while introducing a delicate wash of color.

Paint and wallpaper brand Little Greene selected Marle as its 2027 Color of the Year. Mirroring the return of understated browns in both fashion and interior design, this earthy hue is designed to foster comfort and soften the surrounding space. Describing the shade as “sumptuous and impactful, yet relaxed and grounded,” Little Greene’s Creative Director Ruth Mottershead highlights the balance between richness and restraint that defines the color.

Photography courtesy of California Paints

Quiet Peace by California Paints encourages us to slow down and take in our surroundings. This deep, moody blue creates a sense of calm while leaving room for self-expression through design. Rather than simply decorating a room, Quiet Peace helps create a thoughtful relationship between space, color, and the people who inhabit it, offering a welcome refuge from the chaos of the outside world.

Dunn-Edwards announced French Press Coffee as its 2027 Color of the Year, predicting the rise of desaturated, atmospheric hues in the coming year. This silky, rich brown evokes luxury and history, cultivating intimacy through warmth and understated sophistication.

As the name suggests, Dutch Boy Paints’ Color of the Year, Deep Rooted, aims to create a grounded, authentic, and dependable environment within a space. With its earthy terracotta hue and warm undertones, Deep Rooted encourages personalized, lived-in environments.

Ace Hardware’s paint brand Clark+Kensington chose Vantage Point, a radiant blue shade that brings a crisp, refreshing perspective to interiors and adds uplifting energy to everyday spaces.

Inspired by the natural minerals and pigments found in Italian clay deposits, Graham & Brown’s Rose Earth connects modern spaces with historical painting practices. This subtle pink, enriched by a warm terracotta undertone, brings centuries-old influences into contemporary interiors.

C2’s 2027 Color of the Year, Pueblo, looks beyond simply finding a retreat and explores what it means to feel truly alive within a space. With its earthy pink and mauve tones, Pueblo encourages physical interaction with our surroundings, transforming interiors into spaces where people actively engage with their environment rather than simply retreat from it.

From Drifting Lights to Crystal Grid, Preciosa’s extensive design portfolio brings the craft to customizable crystal.

Abdul El-Sayed Is Winning at Watches, Too

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Michigan voters made history Tuesday night by electing Abdul El-Sayed to the US Senate, as part of a greater blue wave that swept the nation. The 44-year-old former epidemiologist is set to become America’s first Muslim senator—and, in a slightly more trivial milestone, might also be the first real-deal watch nerd to reach the Capitol.

During his victory speech, the senator-elect sported a Grand Seiko Spring Drive Chronograph (ref. SBGC249), a limited-edition piece from the Japanese watchmaker that pairs a GMT (which tracks the time across multiple time zones) and a chronograph (i.e. a stopwatch function). Politicians tend to go one of two ways with their timepieces: Either they’ll cosplay as a man of the people with an affordable Timex or Casio, or they’ll sport a luxury watch with historical pedigree—like the Rolex “President” Day-Date, worn by several commanders-in-chief—to make themselves appear ready for higher office. An obscure Grand Seiko fits neither of those categories.

Instead, El-Sayed’s Grand Seiko is a burly sport model made by one of the world’s most elegant watchmakers. At 43.5mm, it’s hefty, a good fit for the senator-elect’s beefed-up forearms. It’s quite complicated, with several different functions, and a dial as busy as Little Caesars Arena during a Detroit Pistons game. It costs $12,400, but you’d have to be very deep into the hobby to recognize its value.

What I find so compelling about El-Sayed’s choice is that it doesn’t appear to be masquerading as some political symbol. It’s simply the excellent wristwear of an honest-to-goodness collector. “Folks who know me know I’m a sucker for automatic watches and sort of classic watches,” El-Sayed said in an interview with LGBT Detroit in May.

Throughout his campaign, he’s worn a wide range of pieces. His recent go-to appears to be a Mühle-Glashütte S.A.R. Rescue-Timer, a great-looking and obscure German tool watch. He also owns the U-1 SE, a large dive watch from the German independent brand Sinn.

Not all of El-Sayed’s timepieces are devoid of political meaning, however. The senator-elect has worn an Omega Speedmaster, the watch worn by American astronauts Buzz Aldrin and Neil Armstrong when they first set foot on the moon. That’s about as patriotic as a watch can get without excerpting the Declaration of Independence on the dial. Naturally, he also owns a Shinola Runwell, from the brand based in El-Sayed’s native Detroit. El-Sayed is very aware of what a certain watch can signal about the wearer. In his 2020 book, Healing Politics: A Doctor’s Journey into the Heart of Our Political Epidemic, he wrote about how seeing Rolex billboards along a highway prompted him to reflect on income inequality: “How many people who drive this road can actually afford one of those?”

But when did I realize that El-Sayed was a true-blue watch guy? It wasn’t the dialed-in collection of interesting tool watches that did it, but his appearance at a rally on Sunday. He wasn’t wearing a timepiece at all, surprisingly. Which meant that there, on his wrist, was the mark of a true collector: a watch tan.

Pangram’s new models spot AI fakes in images and video, it says

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

The AI text detection startup adds heatmaps, video uploads and browser alerts, but its accuracy figures are self-reported.

Pangram’s demo: a real photo of a restaurant whose wall mural is AI-generated (left), the Image 1.0 heatmap (centre) and the overlay (right).

Pangram launched Pangram Image 1.0 for AI images and the faster Image Flash 1.0 for video. It claims near-perfect scores on its own tests and 98.7% on the DF26 academic benchmark. Both are in its web app and API, and Image 1.0 is in its Chrome extension.

Pangram, a New York startup known for spotting AI-written text, has launched two models for detecting AI images and video, the company said on Wednesday. Pangram Image 1.0 checks still images, while the smaller, faster Pangram Image Flash 1.0 checks video frame by frame.

The image model can also draw a heatmap showing where AI content sits in a picture, such as a fake object added to a real photo. Pangram says it catches 99.2% of AI images and wrongly flags 0.27% of real ones.

In its own test, Pangram made AI copies of 100 real photos with 12 leading image generators, using prompts that closely matched each original. Image 1.0 scored 99.96% accuracy on that set. The test was designed by Pangram, not by outside reviewers.

For video, Pangram used DF26, an academic benchmark of 2,691 clips of people speaking to camera, published in September. Its authors found that both people and leading detectors did little better than chance at telling real clips from fakes.

Pangram says Flash 1.0 scores 98.7% on DF26, 29 points ahead of the next-best detector.

The new models also work on smaller images, down to 256 by 256 pixels from 512 by 512. Results below 512 pixels carry a lower-confidence warning. Pangram says it trains the models to see through compression, blur and noise, which platforms and people trying to fool detectors can add.

Image 1.0 now runs in Pangram’s Chrome extension, which flags AI content on social media, shopping and rental sites. Users can also upload videos in the web app, and the API now handles both images and video. Platforms are cracking down too: Snapchat has stopped recommending AI videos on Spotlight.

Pangram raised $9M in a round led by Menlo Ventures in July, when it first previewed image detection. Its text detector already runs on Substack.

AI detectors still make mistakes. In August, Pangram’s text tool flagged a Berkeley professor’s op-ed as partly AI-written, which she disputed.

Even Meta’s own image detector missed cropped fakes from its generator more than half the time in Reuters tests. On YouTube, a network that paid real actors to read AI scripts beat its detectors.

With expertise in digital marketing, product management, and branding & identity, Ana Maria Constantin develops strategies that resonate (show all) With expertise in digital marketing, product management, and branding & identity, Ana Maria Constantin develops strategies that resonate with our target audience in the software/SaaS industry. Collaboration and teamwork are paramount to her, as she loves empowering her colleagues to achieve outstanding results and unlock their full potential.

Democratic physicians, nurses, and scientists won dramatic victories across the U.S. on Tuesday, part of the sweeping blue wave that saw Democrats regain control of Congress in midterm elections that spotlighted  health care, public health, and trust in science.

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Ally McCoist was critical of one Liverpool player for his unwitting role in Fenerbahce’s equaliser against the Reds in the first half of their Champions League game on Wednesday.

The away side took a well-deserved lead in the 25th minute when Trey Nyoni’s deflected shot sailed into the net, with the teenage midfielder being credited with his first official goal in senior football.

However, the hosts restored parity just four minutes later, and again it was with the aid of a deflection as Mason Greenwood’s free kick hit off Jeremy Jacquet and was diverted into the net, with Alisson Becker not disguising his rage at the defensive wall in front of him.

Speaking on co-commentary for TNT Sports (via BBC Sport), McCoist called out the French defender for turning his back on the ball as the set piece was struck.

The summer arrival from Rennes has been excellent in his time at Liverpool so far, rightly receiving plenty of praise for his performances and winning his first senior call-up for France, but he won’t be happy with his part in Greenwood’s rather fortuitous equaliser.

At the time of writing, hopefully the centre-back and his team can put that regrettable moment behind them and go on to take all three points home to Merseyside.

One Autism Mutation Collapses the Whole Gene Network

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

A genetic investigation led by researchers at Boston Children’s Hospital and Harvard University resolves a longstanding puzzle in neurodevelopmental genomics: how hundreds of distinct genetic mutations can produce the shared biological features of autism. Published in Nature, the study reveals a phenomenon termed “transcriptomic convergence,” where mutating or downregulating a single autism risk gene triggers a coordinated downregulation across an entire network of related autism genes.

Furthermore, the team discovered that an X-linked transcriptional activator called ZFX, which escapes X-chromosome inactivation and is expressed at higher levels in females, acts as a master regulator of this convergent network—uncovering the molecular machinery underlying the female protective effect in autism.

Source: Boston Children’s Hospital / Nature

Over the past two decades, genomic sequencing projects have identified hundreds of rare genetic variants and de novo mutations associated with autism spectrum disorder. However, this vast genetic heterogeneity has posed a fundamental biological paradox: how can distinct disruptions scattered across hundreds of unrelated genes with vastly different molecular functions result in similar neurodevelopmental outcomes and shared behavioral traits?

Concurrently, clinicians and geneticists have grappled with the “female protective effect”, the consistent observation that autism is diagnosed four to five times more frequently in males than in females, and that females often require a substantially higher burden of deleterious genetic mutations before manifesting clinical symptoms.

Now, a study published in Nature by researchers at Boston Children’s Hospital, the Howard Hughes Medical Institute (HHMI), and Harvard Medical School delivers a unified explanation for both phenomena. The team demonstrated that autism genes do not operate in isolation but function within tightly interconnected transcriptional networks where single faults cascade, and identified an X-linked master activator that shields female neural cells from system collapse.

Led by senior author Christopher Walsh, MD, PhD, chief of the Division of Genetics and Genomics at Boston Children’s Hospital, alongside co-lead authors Rebecca Andersen, PhD, and Maya Talukdar, PhD, the researchers investigated human cellular models engineered to carry disruptions in individual, validated autism risk genes.

Rather than observing unique, disjointed transcriptional profiles for each genetic perturbation, the investigators observed a common signature across diverse variants:

“The point is, if you down-regulate one autism gene, you down-regulate a whole bunch of them,” said Dr. Andersen. “This ‘transcriptomic convergence,’ as we call it, gives us a way to understand how hundreds of different genes can cause similar phenotypes, a similar condition.”

This network fragility indicates that autism-related genes act in concert. Rather than requiring cumulative multi-gene hits, a single rare loss-of-function mutation can trigger a molecular domino effect that destabilizes an entire developmental program.

Having identified this interconnected network, the investigators sought to determine what molecular mechanisms might safeguard females from the cascade.

While human females carry two X chromosomes (XX) and males carry one (XY), cellular dosage compensation typically silences most genes on one female X chromosome through X-chromosome inactivation (XCI) to prevent toxic overexpression. However, roughly 15% of X-linked genes escape this silencing.

The team turned their attention to ZFX (zinc finger protein, X-linked), a known transcriptional activator residing on the X chromosome that reliably escapes inactivation. Because both copies remain functionally active, female cells express substantially higher baseline concentrations of the ZFX protein than male cells.

Molecular assays revealed that ZFX directly regulates and drives the expression of numerous genes embedded within the convergent autism network:

“The most important take-home messages are the idea of this transcriptomic convergence in autism and our identification of the ZFX gene as a potential driver of autism’s male bias,” said Dr. Walsh.

The identification of transcriptomic convergence and the ZFX regulatory axis fundamentally reframes the therapeutic horizon for neurodevelopmental conditions.

Historically, the presence of hundreds of unique autism-linked mutations suggested that drug developers would need to engineer hundreds of distinct gene-replacement or gene-editing therapies—a prohibitive challenge for clinical translation.

Because the network converges onto shared nodes, targeting master regulators like ZFX could offer a unified therapeutic strategy. Artificially elevating ZFX expression or activating its downstream targets in males could potentially confer the same neurodevelopmental resilience that females naturally possess. Furthermore, boosting ZFX could also benefit females carrying exceptionally high mutation loads.

As Dr. Walsh noted, developing interventions to amplify the expression of these convergent hubs could represent a therapeutic strategy where “a rising tide lifts all boats.”

What is transcriptomic convergence in autism?

Transcriptomic convergence is a biological phenomenon where disrupting or mutating a single autism-linked gene causes a widespread, coordinated downregulation across an entire network of other autism-associated genes. This explains how hundreds of different rare genetic mutations lead to similar neurobiological outcomes.

How does the ZFX gene protect females from autism?

ZFX is an X-linked transcriptional activator that escapes standard X-chromosome inactivation, meaning females express it at higher levels from both X chromosomes. Because ZFX directly drives the expression of many autism-associated genes, its higher concentration in females acts as a protective buffer, keeping the gene network stable even when a mutation is present.

What are the therapeutic implications of these findings?

Instead of creating individual gene therapies for hundreds of different autism mutations, researchers can focus on developing therapies that upregulate central hubs like ZFX. Boosting ZFX could potentially elevate the entire downstream network of autism genes, extending the natural female protective effect to males.

Funding: This work was supported by the Autism Speaks Postdoctoral Fellowship 13008 and NIGMS T32 GM007748 (R.E.A.); NIH T32 GM007753 and T32 GM144273 (M.T.); the Simons Foundation Autism Research Initiative, the Howard Hughes Medical Institute, Autism BrainNet (953759), the Yang Tan Collective at Harvard University, the Hock E. Tan and K. Lisa Yang Center for Autism Research at Harvard University. D.C.P. and C.A.W. are Investigators of the Howard Hughes Medical Institute.

Transcriptomic convergence and the female protective effect in autism

Autism spectrum disorder (ASD) is highly heritable, and mutations in hundreds of genes have been implicated as individually rare causes of ASD. Understanding how disruptions to these functionally diverse genes lead to the core features of ASD remains a major challenge.

Moreover, ASD is three- to four-fold more common in male than in female individuals, and female autistic people tend to carry more autosomal risk alleles for ASD compared with their male counterparts; however, the biological basis of this ‘female protective effect’ (FPE) has remained unknown.

Here we show that individual perturbations of 18 ASD genes in human neural progenitor cells converge on shared effects on gene expression, including widespread downregulation of other ASD genes. De novo reconstruction of a gene regulatory network enabled identification of central transcriptional regulators that drive this transcriptomic convergence, including prominent ASD gene CHD8 and candidates including REST.

Furthermore, X-linked transcription factor ZFX, which is expressed from both the active and the inactive X chromosomes in female individuals, emerged as a key activator of many ASD genes: we propose that the higher ZFX expression level observed in the female brain can buffer damaging mutations in diverse ASD genes, contributing to the female protective effect.

Together, these results show how key gene regulatory networks can become broadly and similarly dysregulated upon disruption of individual ASD genes and provide molecular insight into the female protective effect in ASD.

9 Easy (Enough) Ways to Toddler-Proof Your Christmas Tree

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

This one’s a no-brainer. Pack up your heirloom ornaments for the foreseeable future and invest in some chic shatterproof options. Think of it as a chance to temporarily lean into a fun trend, like a monochromatic vibe or a brightly colored artificial tree with fewer ornaments (make it extra fun by letting your kids pick the color—if they can agree, ha).

The good thing about toddlers is that they’re small, so simply placing a shorter tree up on a table is enough to keep those curious hands at bay. Make sure your platform is high enough that your tot can’t tug a branch and pull the whole tree down, and heavy enough that they can’t topple the table itself.

The other great thing about toddlers is that they’re easily duped, so if you wrap a few big boxes in festive paper and position them around the base of your tree, they look like the other presents that are hands-off until Christmas morning. Throw some heavy stuff in the boxes to keep them stable (anything you have lying around your house works). If you’re not as Pinterest-y as this sounds (same), throw a baby gate around the tree. (This one is actually not ugly!)

To keep your evergreen firmly planted on the ground, grab a few bricks (you can wrap them all pretty if you like) and place them on the legs or base of your tree to really hold it in place. This means if your toddler somehow makes a break for it, they won’t be able to tip it over.

Mentally place your ornaments in a pyramid of preciousness, with the most cherished at the top and the least delicate (those “charming” homemade or felted ones) at the bottom. This way, your tot’s curious little hands can only grab ornaments that won’t break or hurt them. You could also consider going all felt for a few years or only decorating the top of the tree.

Any toddler parent knows that when the house is too quiet, disaster looms, so tie some jingle bell ornaments to the lower branches to give you a heads-up if your kid’s made contact with the tree. Bonus: they look quite festive.

If the lower branches are the biggest liability, go ahead and chop them off.  Problem solved.

Check your older ornaments to see if any still hang with a sharp metal hook and update them with a loop of ribbon or ornament anchors.  That way, if your kiddo does get their hands on one of the shiny things hanging from the tree, you know they won’t accidentally poke themselves.

There are tons of tree options specifically made for toddlers if you’re OK giving up the greenery for a few years. Or rock two Christmas trees so your toddler has their own safe version to manhandle. Here are a few outside-the-box ideas:

If you ever went through a washi tape phase, create a cute and toddler-proof Christmas tree by taping the right shape to the wall (with no harm to your paint!). Kids can help decorate with ribbons of washi and round pieces of paper acting as ornaments. I know it’s not the same effect, but at least you’ll be able to rest easy.

If your house can somehow fit one more toy, add this Step 2 toddler Christmas tree to the mix. It’s the perfect size for your merry little munchkin; they can redecorate it as many times as they like, and there’s a fun train track going around the bottom so it’s a gift that keeps on giving.

If space is tight but you’ve got a free wall, this Project Montessori toddler-proof felt Christmas tree will keep them busy while you work on the main event. The felt cutout is about 3 feet tall, perfect for 3-year-olds who want to decorate their tree again and again and again and again—and with 26 Velcro ornaments, they’ll never run out of combinations.

Bringing a little more joy to parenthood.

United Airlines First Class Steak And Eggs: Great Steak, But Where Were The Eggs?

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

United Airlines served me excellent cubed steak for breakfast on a recent flight from Chicago to Houston. The problem with its steak and eggs dish? The missing eggs.

For this week’s Meal of the Week, I want to highlight a breakfast that I would happily order again, even though one part of it was very disappointing. On a recent flight from Chicago O’Hare (ORD) to Houston (IAH), I selected the steak and eggs, described on the menu as:

Steak and eggs with home fries and chimichurri sauce

The tray was nicely presented, with sliced steak topped with chimichurri, potatoes, and what I expected would be an egg soufflé.

The steak was excellent for airplane red meat. The chimichurri worked very well with the beef, and this was the highlight of the meal.

Unfortunately, instead of the egg soufflé I expected, I received something much closer to bread pudding…in fact, it was the same bread pudding I had out of Chicago a couple months ago. There may have been egg binding it together, but it had virtually no discernible egg texture or flavor. It was like eating bread.

You can see the bread cubes in the photo. This was not simply a soufflé that turned out a little firmer than expected. Bread dominated the portion that was supposed to provide the eggs in my steak and eggs.

That was disappointing, especially because the steak was so good and because I try to limit my carbs. I hope United can address this with its catering out of Chicago, as I’ve seen other reports of the same problem (this wasn’t a one-off). An actual egg component would make this a much more satisfying breakfast and match the menu…

The accompaniments were better. The chocolate croissant was served warm and was excellent. Between that and the bread pudding, I certainly had enough bread on the tray…

The fruit was ripe, and the Chobani vanilla Greek yogurt was decent. I would prefer a no-sugar-added Greek yogurt, but at least this one provided 10 grams of protein.

The coffee was strong, which was also appreciated. I also requested orange juice, though I must confess that was more to add color to the tray for the photo. I did not drink it.

I would order United’s steak and eggs again. The steak with chimichurri was excellent, and the warm chocolate croissant was great. But United needs to fix the egg issue out of ORD. There is a good breakfast here. It just needs the eggs its name promises.

Nintendo's main Switch cartridge supplier is leaving the ROM business

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

This is potentially terrible news for those who love physical media.

Nintendo's main Switch cartridges has plans to abandon the ROM portion of its business, according to reporting by the Taipei Times. The Taiwanese semiconductor and memory manufacturer Macronix is phasing out its ROM production, which was confirmed by CEO Miin Wu. This could spell bad news for gamers who love physical media.

Macronix just announced a record-high net profit of $337 million for Q3 2026, but Wu says many of its new customers have issued a "demand for NOR used in data centers." NOR is a type of flash memory.

1/4 Nintendo's main memory chip maker is quitting the Switch business Macronix, which makes the ROM chips in Switch 1 and Switch 2 game memory cards, is phasing out that business. There will be no new ROM products, and Q3 is the last quarter it reports ROM sales separately.

— David Gibson (@gibbogame) November 4, 2026

The company's ROM business has actually declined by 39 percent over the past year. This section of Macronix now accounts for just five percent of sales, down from 26 percent this time last year. In other words, the financials just aren't there.

There is a silver lining here. Macronix may be Nintendo's main supplier of ROM for its cartridges, but it's far from the only entity helping to create physical Switch cartridges. Also, this doesn't impact Switch 2 cartridges in quite the same way. That's because the Switch 2 carts use NAND flash memory, a market segment that Macronix isn't phasing out.

Sony PlayStation FY2025 Q4 Financial Results!

• PS5 units sold – 1.5M • Lifetime PS5 units – 93.7M • Full game software units – 74.6M • First party software units – 5.8M • Digital Download Ratio – 85% • Monthly Active Users – 125M pic.twitter.com/ARBwBehu6b

However, this doesn't look too good for the long-term sustainability of physical game cartridges and discs. Digital products already account for 61.5 percent of Nintendo's software sales. This is even starker when you look at the other console manufacturers. Digital downloads make up around 85 percent of software sales on the PS5, which could account for Sony's recent move to end physical media sales altogether.

Both of the above numbers pale in comparison to Microsoft and its Xbox division. A whopping 96 percent of software sales are digital on Xbox platforms. The company has still yet to announce a plan to sunset physical media.

Share of Adjustable Rate Mortgages Hits Highest Level Since September 2025

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

A house key sitting on mortgage application.To see more of my financial images click on the link below:

Mortgage rates ticked down a bit this past week but landing at 7.3%, it wasn’t enough to move the needle for buyers submitting loan applications for 30-year-fixed rate mortgages. Sitting almost a full percentage point lower, adjustable rate mortgages (ARMs) however continue to be attractive for buyers looking to start out with a lower monthly payment.

The latest Market Composite Index from the Mortgage Bankers Association (MBA)—its measure of mortgage loan activity volume and includes purchases and refinances—shows mortgage application activity decreasing 1.2% on a seasonally adjusted basis after last week’s 3.4% increase. On an unadjusted basis, the Index decreased 2% compared with the previous week.

This week’s decline follows weeks of fluctuations as the 30-year fixed mortgage rate still lingers around 7.29%, according to Freddie Mac and this week currently sits at 7.25%, according to Mortgage News Daily. Freddie Mac’s next rate report will be released Thursday.

“Mortgage rates drifted down to their lowest level since September 2026 last week in the wake of the Federal Reserve’s decision to hold on additional rate hikes at its October meeting,” said Mike Fratantoni, MBA’s SVP and chief economist. “While the 30-year rate declined to 7.3 percent, activity is still quite slow, with purchase application volume down for the week and refinance volume effectively flat at a very low level.”

MBA once again pointed to increased activity in the ARM share of total mortgage applications as a sign that buyers are ready to move on lower rates. According to the report, the average contract interest rate for 5/1 ARMs decreased to 6.29% from 6.41%, with points decreasing to 1.00 from 1.20 (including the origination fee) for 80 percent LTV loans. The adjustable-rate mortgage (ARM) share of activity increased to 11.4 percent of total applications, MBA reported.

“As we have highlighted in recent weeks, more borrowers are choosing ARMs in this environment to lower their initial payments, with the ARM share at its highest level since September 2025,” Fratantoni said.

An ARM loan is where the interest rate is fixed for an initial period, typically five years, and then changes annually for the life of the loan, based on market conditions.

MBA’s Refinance Index increased 0.1% from the previous week and was 60% lower than the same week one year ago. MBA’s seasonally adjusted Purchase Index–its weekly measurement of nationwide home loan applications based on a sample of about 75% of U.S. mortgage activity–decreased 2% from one week earlier. The unadjusted Purchase Index decreased 4% compared with the previous week and was 19% lower than the same week one year ago, according to the report.

The refinance share of mortgage activity saw an increase this week–to 39.8% from 38.9% the previous week, MBA reported.

A look at government-backed loans this week shows the FHA share of total applications decreased to 16.0% from 16.6% the week prior. The VA share of total applications increased to 11.2% from 11.0% the week prior, and the USDA share of total applications increased to 0.6% from 0.5% the week prior.

The Best Fundamental Kettlebell Exercises for Beginners to Master

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

These are the cornerstone movements you need to train with these useful gym tools.

SOME OF THE most powerful, effectivekettlebell exercises rely on a few basic movements. Follow our practice recommendations to familiarize yourself with these building blocks, and they’ll become second nature in no time. Once you’ve mastered these, you’ll be able to tackle the more complex stuff—and supersize your gains.

These are the types of movements you'll learn in No Gym Required: Kettlebells, an MH training guide. You'll learn everything from the fundamentals for beginners to more advanced techniques and workout plans.

Want to get a jumpstart on your kettlebell practice? Men's Health MVP Premium members have exclusive access to a 14-day training program straight from the book, so you can kick off your workouts with expert guidance. Here's how you can get it.

Why: This move teaches you the hip hinge, which is an essential movement pattern that’s instrumental in making you powerful—and just may help you avoid lower back pain too. You should hinge whenever you bend to pick something up from the floor, pushing your butt back while keeping your lower back flat and your torso rigid. This protects your lower back while also setting you up to transfer force efficiently from the floor to your upper body. It’s key in kettlebell training: Whenever you deadlift, clean, snatch, squat, or row correctly, you’re also hinging. Take it slowly as you learn it. Later, you’ll be able to generate maximum power whether you’re holding a kettlebell, swinging a golf club, or punching a heavy bag.

Key Performance Points: Bend at the waist and not at the knees. Instead of visualizing your chest going down and up, focus on pushing your butt backward, then bring it forward. The closer you can keep the kettlebell to your body, the more effective these moves will be.

Sets and Reps: Do 3 sets of 10 to 12 reps. Focus on feeling your glutes. Rest 60 seconds between each set.

Why: The swing is one of the most popular and effective kettlebell moves, so mastering the finer points of the form is a must for anyone looking to add the implement to their repertoire. The key here is making sure to emphasize the hip hinge—when you lower the weight down, be sure not to squat before thrusting to snap the weight up. The hips are among your body’s most powerful joints. They’re also among the most mobile. Learning to contract your glutes and extend your hip joints forcefully is foundational to just about any athletic movement you can think of, and it all starts with the swing.

Key Performance Point: Don’t use your shoulders to drive the bell upward. Think of your arms like rope—they just attach your body to the bell. Your legs will create the power to swing upward. Focus on glute drive. The faster and more powerfully you stand and squeeze your glutes, the more easily the bell will swing upward. Keep your abs super-tight. That will protect your spine as the bell swings backward on each rep.

Sets and Reps: Aim to do 10 to 15 reps in 45 seconds, then rest for 15 seconds. Repeat for 5 sets.

Why: What happens when you combine a hip hinge and a front rack? It’s a little more complicated than just putting one and two together, but you’ll finish with a kettlebell clean. As we mentioned above, the hinge transfers power from the floor to your upper body with maximal efficiency. Cleaning the kettlebell is the next logical step: After you propel the bell upward with your glutes and hamstrings, your shoulder and forearm take over as you roll your wrist around and catch the bell in the rack position. Back in the days of the old-time strongmen, a lift wasn’t complete unless you moved a heavy object from the floor to overhead. The clean gets you most of the way there.

Key Performance Points: Thumb to chest. If you feel the kettlebell slamming into you, guide it more. Try a drill: Stand facing a wall and perform cleans. This will remind you not to swing the kettlebell out in front of you. Keep your thumb pointed in. This facilitates the roll around the wrist of the kettlebell into the rack position to finish the clean.

Why: The kettlebell rack position isn’t quite a standalone exercise either—but it’s an essential technique for holding the implement for all types of exercises, from lunges to carries. Mastering the rack position is fundamental to other kettlebell moves because it forces you to control and stabilize the weight in many directions at once. Taming the kettlebell in this way develops the grip, core, and glute strength you’ll need when you start pressing, swinging, and cleaning these unruly implements as the workouts grow in complexity.

Key Performance Points: The interior portion of the forearm should be pressed against your chest, which solves incorrect elbow position (it should be pointing down). Do not straighten your wrist. It should be in a strong flexed position, which will take pressure off your elbow. Keep your shoulders packed down. Your shoulder blades should be pulled down and back as opposed to lifted up like you’re shrugging (which will overtax the upper trap).

Sets and Reps: Start with single-arm front racks, doing each arm individually. Clean the bell into place, and hold for 30 seconds. Do 3 sets per side.

Why: Here, you’re working to push the weight overhead. Pressing a kettlebell is fundamental and you can start by doing it from a standing position. Learning to properly overhead press the bell does a few things. First off, it teaches you to own the overhead press position, which will set you up for overhead carries, windmills, getups, and other moves that will make kettlebell training even more fun. It also builds shoulder stability and increases your core stability.

Key Performance Points: Start from a good rack position. Keep your wrist forward at the top. Keep your biceps close to your ear, and keep a solid foundation by flexing your lats and shoulder blades.

Sets and Reps: Do 3 sets of 8 to 10 reps per side. Rest minimally between each set.

Why: Finally, you’ll use a hip hinge to bring the kettlebell overhead. This is the biggest test of your ability to generate power from the floor, and to coordinate the action of your upper and lower body. Learn to do it well and other strength and athletic movements will feel easy by comparison.

Key Performance Points: Don’t swing the kettlebell too far out in front of you. Guide the kettlebell even closer to your body and higher with your high pull. Once it’s level with your head, you’re done pulling—now you’re punching, getting your hand up to that locked-out position.

Sets and Reps: Do 10 reps, switch arms, and repeat.

Why: The basic halo is an incredibly effective movement that builds shoulder stability while working the core. It strengthens your mind-muscle connection, requiring full concentration and a team effort from your shoulders, triceps, back, and core to maneuver the unwieldy weight around your head. As a result, you can increase focus, boost intensity, and get the most out of every single rep. There’s more. In most gym movements, the weight follows a linear path. The halo is what exercise physiologists call a “transverse plane”—or rotational—exercise, which forces you to initiate movement and stabilize all 360 degrees of your core muscles, much as you do in life and sport. Over time, transverse plane moves build a tougher, more resilient core that is able to withstand shocks and absorb impact from all directions.

Key Performance Points: Keep your abs tight! The halo is harder than you think, and very easily can become a hula dance, with your hips moving and your lower back arching. Offset this by tightening your abs to pull your ribcage in. Don’t rush. Work the bell around your head slowly.

Sets and Reps: Try the exercise for 10 to 20 reps per direction to start before adding extra challenges, like the kneeling position or a squat, for a bigger metabolic impact.

Why: This compound exercise isn't just for the barbell. You'll hone your hip hinge using a kettlebell to deadlift, which can be a great reason for a beginner to add the movement to their routine. You're working the same movement as the good morning—you'll just change the position of the load to pick it up from the floor (which winds up being much more practical, both in your training and IRL).

Key Performance Points: Don't look up. As you lift the weight from the floor, keep your gaze trained on a spot on the floor just in front of you. This will help to keep your neck in a neutral position, which is important for spine health.

Sets and Reps: Start with 8 to 12 reps. You won't be able to load up as heavily with kettlebells as other implements, so focus on mastering the movement pattern here.

Why: This exercise is one of the best ways for beginners to learn how to squat, period. The position of the load helps to reinforce proper posture without being too difficult to handle, reinforcing bracing and spinal stability in ways the back squat doesn't.

Key Performance Points: Don't sweat depth. You'll be in a good position to lower down to the right spot for you; just avoid stopping before you hit parallel.

Sets and Reps: Start with 8 to 12 reps. Like the deadlift, you won't be able to load up as heavily with kettlebells as other implements when you squat, so focus on mastering the movement pattern here.

Why: This might look like you're just walking in place, but the kettlebell march is actually a challenging core exercise. The key is in the position of the load and bracing your abs—which you'll quickly discover is much harder than it looks.

Key Performance Points: Don't rush. Your steps should be slow and deliberate as you fight to keep your hips square and the weight level.

Sets and Reps: You can march for reps or for time. Try for 30 seconds on, 30 seconds off for 3 minutes.

Why: You’re giving your abs a uniquely challenging movement here, addressing both the bracing and rotational aspects of your core. If that weren’t enough, holding the weight overhead is a sneaky-effective way to hone shoulder health, too.

Key Performance Points: Key your eyes locked on the weight overhead throughout the movement. Keep your ribcage tight, and move slowly with intention instead of jerking up and down.

Sets and Reps: Perform 3 sets of 6 to 8 reps per side, making sure to move slowly through each step.

Why: This is your opportunity to build power without as much technical challenge as the snatch. Even better, once you swap kettlebells for the barbell lots of people use for push presses, you’ll be putting your shoulders in a much healthier position.

Key Performance Points: You’re doing a push press, not a thruster. Don’t squat down; keep your depth shallow.

7 Mindful Writing Prompts to Foster a Deeper Connection with Yourself

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

The most trusted resource for mindfulness and meditation.

This is an opportunity for reflection—without judgment. Explore seven writing prompts that encourage us to connect with who we are.

Sometimes the hardest thing about having a mindfulness or meditation practice is just making time. With 24 hours in a day, things to do, places to be, people who need us, things we need to take care of, we all ask ourselves, how do we make time? What I found is I can’t overthink it or it won’t happen.

Take a deep breath right now. That’s the practice. Imagine if every day you found moments to breathe, to reflect, to be more present.

As you pause to take one of those deep breaths, maybe you’re already reflecting on how you’re showing up in the world, what you’re letting people see, and what you’re not letting people see. Where can you begin to be more of yourself?

Dive into self-reflection about who you are—fears, love, things you want to show the world, maybe things you haven’t thought about in a while.

You can write this, you can say it aloud, you can think it quietly. It can even end up looking like a poem if you want. You can also explore these prompts simply as you pause throughout the day to take a deep breath. This is an opportunity to connect with yourself.Finish the sentences.

I’m not a poet, but I’m going to share mine if you’re willing to read it.

3. I come from a challenging childhood and a loving family.

4. I dream of a world where everyone can be themselves without being judged because it doesn’t match what other people think you should be.

5. I fear not being able to speak my truth and having to pretend that I don’t have feelings.

6. I hope for a world where young men and boys learn that they can feel, that feelings are human. I hope for a world where young men and boys don’t have to pretend that they don’t have feelings because it’s not manly, it’s not tough, it’s not strong. I hope strength will be considered as showing your true feelings in a healthy way.

7. I love being able to get up every day and do what I feel I was born to do.

Alaska Atmos Rewards offers the Global Getaways promotion, whereby members can save on award tickets in select regions. Atmos Rewards launches this promotion on the first Wednesday of each month (which the program calls “Atmos Members Day”), so we just saw the one for November launched.

Let’s go over the opportunities, as you can save up to 50% on award tickets. There are some cool markets in which you can save miles, though unfortunately only economy travel qualifies.

For the new Alaska Atmos Rewards Global Getaways promotion, you can save up to 50% on award travel to nine destinations, with the following restrictions:

The theme for this Global Getaways promotion is “lights, camera, departure,” with the idea being to “roll out the red carpet for the destinations that have starred in your favorite films and shows.” With this sale, you can save on one-way redemptions to the following destinations (these savings are up to 50%, but not always that high):

I very much appreciate Alaska Atmos Rewards’ creativity with this promotion, as nowadays it’s pretty rare to see programs offer award sales that are also valid for travel on partner airlines. Now, admittedly I wish there weren’t quite as many restrictions in terms of the booking window, travel period, the class of service limitations, and the one-way discount, but still, there will be value here for some, and it’s better than nothing.

If you’re simply looking for the lowest cost award, some of these award prices are very tough to beat. For example, getting from the mainland to Hawaii for 11,500 points is a great deal, as is getting from the United States to India for 30,000 points. Keep in mind that with Atmos Rewards, award flights even earn status points, so could help you qualify for status.

I do hope that over time we see some first and business class award sales as well (it’s probably not happening, but we can dream, right?). Many of us have historically loved the program for the value it offers for premium cabin travel, so this promotion doesn’t help us much with that.

This is basically Alaska’s version of Air France-KLM Flying Blue Promo Rewards or Singapore KrisFlyer Spontaneous Escapes, except it applies for travel on partner airlines (rather than just for travel on the program’s airline), and is exclusive to economy (while the other promotions are mixed).

Alaska Atmos Rewards has launched its latest Global Getaways promotion, which is a monthly redemption deal offering up to a 50% discount on awards. This time around, you can save points on economy redemptions to select destinations. You need to book by November 7, 2026, mostly for travel in early 2027. While this won’t be useful for everyone, I imagine some people will get value from this.

Montana’s Newest Congressman Dresses Like a Ralph Lauren Model

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

You couldn’t ask for a better embodiment of the blue wave than Sam Forstag, the 32-year-old rookie Montana Democrat who defeated his Republican rival in yesterday’s midterms to flip an important House seat. While unaffordability was a key issue in the midterms across the country, it’s especially noticeable in Montana, where an influx of billionaires and tech companies has sent the cost of living soaring. Like many of his compatriots, Forstag campaigned on promises of affordable housing, better healthcare, and getting money out of politics. Unlike his fellow Democratic challengers, however, he did it in a wardrobe that looked straight off a rack at the RRL store.

Last night, in front of a crowd of supporters in Missoula, Forstag delivered his victory speech dressed in a hickory-striped work shirt, jeans, a big western belt buckle, and a pair of weathered brown forestry boots. Forstag’s fit stands in contrast to the standard election night business-casual look favored by political hopefuls on both sides of the aisle, but he’s far from the first candidate to lean into the dress codes of blue-collar Americans. From Tim Walz’s camo hats and Filson Mackinaw jackets to John Fetterman’s Carhartt hoodie, workwear remains a potent symbol of working-class solidarity. In this case, however, it’s not just blue-collar cosplay.

Forstag at a student get-out-the-vote gathering at Montana State University in October.

Straight out of college and looking to pay off his student loans, Forstag joined the US Forest Service as a wildland firefighter and spent the next eight years battling blazes around the country, including four years as a smokejumper parachuting into fire zones. As a result, he comes by his love of Levi’s and sturdy, Goodyear-welted footwear honestly, and—Montana being a place where jeans and work shirts are considered business-casual for many—didn’t need to change his look for the campaign.

Forstag addressing voters at a town hall in August.

As authentic as Forstag’s election-night outfit was, he also appears to be a guy who understands the power of styling. While his shirt likely didn’t have Ralph Lauren’s initials on the tag (wildland firefighters only make $20 an hour), the way he wore it—half unbuttoned, layered over a red thermal henley, and tucked into his jeans—owes a debt to the romantic take on Western style Lauren has been trafficking in since the ’70s. Or, equally likely, the Montana cowboys who the designer borrowed it from. Either way, Forstag’s look, like the progressive politics he and his Democratic colleagues campaigned on, will be a welcome disruption to business as usual on Capitol Hill.

Dave Challinor says one thing in first Sheffield Wednesday interview that fans are loving

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Sheffield Wednesday's new manager Dave Challinor has given his first interview since taking over at Hillsborough, and one thing that he said in particular has struck a chord with fans.

It's a clear sign of the ambition of the new owners of Sheffield Wednesday that Henrik Pedersen barely lasted into the autumn as their manager. Pedersen was sacked on the 25th October, following two successive 2-0 defeats against Notts County and Doncaster Rovers which left them in 8th place in League One, and it took just a week for the former Stockport County manager Dave Challinor to be appointed as his replacement.

Wednesday have drawn both of their matches since Pedersen's departure, with their last match against Blackpool finishing 1-1 on the 1st November, leaving them in 9th place in League One. Challinor's formal appointment into the position came two days later, with part of the duties of the new manager being to sit down for an interview with the club's media outlet. And there was one thing in particular that he said which delighted the club's fans and demonstrated that he could form a real bond with them.

In an interview with Sheffield Wednesday's media department and posted to the social media platform X, Dave Challinor laid out his ambitions for the club and his intentions for his new team.

He praised the new owners of the club for their "forthright vision" of what they want the club to be and spoke about the importance of the "standards" that are set for their players and the extent to which all the actions that take place behind the scenes at the club can influence what happens when the team takes to the pitch.

How high do you think Sheffield Wednesday could go under Dave Challinor? Let us know in the comments below!

But there was one particular comment that he made which has prompted considerable excitement from Wednesday fans. Asked what his message was for them, he replied by saying, "We're all Wednesday, aren't we," before adding: "We are aligned on what we want the football club to look like. My job, and all of our jobs, really, across the executive leadership, the squad, and all the people off the pitch, is to not only represent the owners, but the supporters and the city of Sheffield."

Sheffield Wednesday have moved to appoint Dave Challinor as Henrik Pedersen's replacement.

Dave Challinor's first interview as the new manager of Sheffield Wednesday has prompted a strong response from fans, so here's a little selection of what's been said about him.

This Wednesdayite was delighted with how straightforward Challinor was throughout the interview:

And this fan is excited for a fresh start with a new man in charge:

This Stockport County fan confirmed that Wednesday have made the right choice with their new manager:

And that was a sentiment echoed by this supporter of Hartlepool United, where Challinor was the manager from 2019 to 2021:

If Dave Challinor wanted to build an immediate bond with Sheffield Wednesday fans, using the phrase "We're all Wednesday aren't we" couldn't have been a more potent one to use. Often abbreviated to "WAWAW", it's become a rallying cry for the club's long-suffering supporters although, as this thread from the Wednesday forum Owlstalk demonstrates, its origins aren't completely clear, though they could stretch back as far as the 1970s.

Challinor arrives at a club that is in a hurry to get back to the Championship following their relegation at the end of the 2025-26 season. Henrik Pedersen was given an opportunity to prove himself again this season, but was unable to find the consistency required to keep the club near the top of the table, and the new manager's first task will be to get the team back to winning ways.

There are reasons to be optimistic that he'll be able to do so. Although results were patchy enough to result in Pedersen's dismissal, there have already been clearly visible green shoots of recovery this season, not least their 7-2 win against Bromley in August and a 5-0 win against Wimbledon in the middle of October. But the Wimbledon win is Wednesday's only one in their last eight League matches, and their slide down to 9th place in the table is one that needs to be arrested.

Barnsley 0-1 Sheffield Wednesday (after extra time), Wembley, 29 May 2023. Type the surnames of Darren Moore's starting XI. Every player you get drops onto the pitch.

You have 90 seconds. Surnames are enough.

Josh Windass headed in Lee Gregory's cross in the 123rd minute to send the Owls up.

The good news for Challinor is that there's plenty of time to do so, with more than two-thirds of the season left to play, while the tightness of the division this season - no-one in League One has won more than eight of their opening fourteen fixtures of the season so far - means that his team still only three points off 4th place and six points off second. Ironically, that second automatic promotion place is currently held by Stockport County, who Challinor left to go to Hillsborough in the first place.

Under new ownership who are clearly emotionally invested in the club, and with attendances already averaging over 31,000, the building blocks are in place for Sheffield Wednesday to make a quick return to the Championship. Dave Challinor's job now is to build the team to match that investment and that support. There are never any guarantees of success in football, as Wednesday fans know all too well from their own recent history, but they've put themselves in a strong position to achieve that off the pitch. Now it's time to prove it on the pitch, as well.

Google’s latest smart home products make the perfect gifts, and they’re on sale at Best Buy

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Google Home is one of the most user-friendly smart home ecosystems, and Google has built out an extensive collection of cameras, speakers, and displays. That positions them as great gifts to expand someone’s smart home.

It’s pretty common to get one or two smart home devices and call it a day, but ecosystems like Google Home begin to come alive when there are several devices working in harmony with each other. For instance, you can start out with the Google Nest Learning Thermostat (4th gen), as many do. It makes A/C and heat controls easy, and even saves you money along the way.

But adding something like the Google Home Speaker with Gemini built-in means you get even more control of your home’s indoor environment. You could go a step further and add a Google Nest Cam (indoor or outdoor) or a Google Nest Doorbell, whether wired or battery. It’s a bit pricier, but I love the Google Nest Cam with Floodlight as a smart home gift option for someone, since it provides more utility.

In any case, adding a couple more foundational pieces to your smart home makes it more useful. And as Google adds features with Gemini for Home, users get access to smart alerts and notifications that those cameras didn’t provide a year or two ago, all within the Google Home app.

If you’re gifting for someone who doesn’t have the beginnings of a smart home setup, Google’s Nest products are a good start. You could start with the Google Home Speaker, depending on their needs. If they like listening to music and want a smart home assistant at the ready, Google’s latest speaker delivers.

The Google Home Speaker has a 360-degree speaker that throws audio in every direction, filling the room. It controls other devices as that smart home fills out, and it can even act as part of a stereo setup for the Google TV Streamer.

Right now, the Google Home Speaker is at its lowest price on Best Buy, sitting at $79.99 – $20 off. It comes in some great colorways, too, like Berry and Jade. Of course, there’s Porcelain and Hazel for more toned-down looks.

Another great smart home starter gift is the Nest Learning Thermostat (4th gen) or Nest Thermostat. The difference between the two comes down to look and capabilities, but both work very well.

The Nest Learning Thermostat (4th gen) works with the majority of HVAC systems and converts them into one manageable system through the Google Home app. Over time, it learns your heating and cooling habits to form better schedules that save you money and stress.

‘Wildwood’ Director Travis Knight on How Live-Action Filmmaking Shaped His Stop-Motion Animated Adventure

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Many scenes in “Wildwood” are designed to look spectacular. Even if each frame weren’t already rendered with the meticulous detail and artistry of stop-motion animation, the Laika Studios film is full of complex, chaotic battle sequences, a forest writhing with eldritch magic, and giant eagles soaring through the sky.

But some of the most technically challenging scenes, according to director Travis Knight, aren’t the ones that look the most majestic. They are — true to the spirit of the film and the lesson learned by its stubborn, caring protagonist Prue (Peyton Elizabeth Lee) — the simple magic of everyday life. Related Stories ‘Club Kid’ Review: Jordan Firstman’s Brazen, Funny, and Surprisingly Earnest Directing Debut Is a Wild Party Movie with Heart ‘Being Heumann’ Review: Siân Heder’s ‘CODA’ Follow-Up Is an Essential Biopic That’s Not Afraid to Have Some Fun

In fact, Knight told IndieWire on an episode of the Filmmaker Toolkit podcast that one of the most challenging sequences in “Wildwood” was simply getting its hero from one end of her bedroom to the other. Laika prefers Canon cameras mounted on large motion-control rigs, which offer the flexibility needed to capture puppet movements adjusted one frame at a time. But when the camera moves, it also has to do so one painstaking frame at a time.

Knight, who’s now directed live-action features “Bumblebee” and “Masters of the Universe” in addition to his Laika work on “Kubo and the Two Strings,” wanted “Wildwood” to have the same camera language as a live-action film. He wanted the intimacy and wordless visual storytelling of a camera tracking Prue as she huffs across her bedroom, where the camera movement helps us see the decor shift from the artifacts of a carefree kid to those of a more disaffected teen.

“I still wanted it to feel like it was shot like a live-action film, with that kind of intimacy with the camera. But a camera on a motion-control rig is the size of a Ford F-150 in our [puppet-scale] world. It’s huge. And so if a camera is moving, you have to create space for the camera on set,” Knight said.

This is on top of creating space for the animator, who is roughly the size of a Kaiju in comparison to the fifth-scale the humans on either side of the Impassable Wood live at. The Prue puppet is roughly 10 inches tall. Being able to move that puppet and a camera at least as large as her bedroom that the puppet is moving through? That is a particularly gnarly feat of physics.

“We essentially had to deconstruct the set,” Knight said. “It was almost like it was a clamshell. It would open to make room for the camera, and then it would close up again. It was such a complicated engineering feat, when all it is [for] is a camera panning across a room. Those are the kinds of challenges that the team has to figure out, which is stupid, but it’s what it requires if you’re trying to tell a story like this.”

There is much to be said, and rightly, about the awe-inducing moments of spectacle in “Wildwood.” The Laika team had to figure out how to make giant puppets out of fabric and feathers instead of standard-issue silicone to simulate flight compellingly, accurately, and tactilely. Combat sequences required enormous attention to detail. But when Knight watches the film, he’s just as struck by the invisible work that makes “Wildwood” feel like a live-action epic — the thankless but involving work that viewers aren’t ever meant to see.

“I learned a great many things about what to do, what not to do [working in live-action]. It fundamentally changed my relationship with the camera, which is one of the reasons why ‘Wildwood’ looks so different. It’s because I wanted it to feel as if it were without the inherent limitations you would typically find in stop-motion cinematography,” Knight said. “When I see this movie, I am seeing all these little miraculous moments of engineering and artistry and human creativity at play on the big screen, and that’s one of the reasons it moves me so much.”

Firefox's New 'Mini Window' Feature Is a Game Changer for Productivity

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

David Nield is a technology journalist from Manchester in the U.K. who has been writing about gadgets and apps for more than 20 years.

He has a bachelor's degree in English Literature from Durham University, where he also spent a term as editor of the award-winning student newspaper Palatinate. His journalism career started in print media, where he contributed to and edited several technology magazines and bookazines sold in the U.K. and internationally. More recently, he has worked as a freelancer for some of the biggest technology publications on the web, covering everything from on-the-ground reporting about product launches, to detailed explainers and how-to guides on apps, gadgets, and platforms. His expertise covers broad areas of consumer tech, including smartphones, laptops, wearables, and AI.

For many of us, web browsing involves juggling dozens of tabs at once, and it's easy for important pages to get lost in the chaos. Now, Firefox has introduced something I think could help us multi-taskers: a "Mini Window." As highlighted by ZDNET's Jack Wallen, this new Firefox feature works like picture-in-picture overlays for videos—only here, that PiP displays a website.

"[Mini Window] lets you move a tab, or a selected section of a page, into a compact window that stays on top of your other windows while you browse," runs Mozilla's official blurb. "It's useful when you're multitasking and need to keep a few sources of information in view at once: Reference docs, a chat, a video call, a form you're filling out." As such, Mini Window doesn't necessarily replace running tabs side-by-side; rather, you can prioritize one tab in a full-size window, while keeping another page in view at all times.

This is a feature that's still in its early stages, and right now you can only get it in the "Nightly" version of Firefox, a very early beta version of the browser. Mozilla says it will push the feature to the full version of the browser later this month.

If you want to try this out as soon as possible, first install Firefox Nightly on your Mac or PC. Once up and running, you can launch Mini Window by right-clicking on a tab header and choosing Move Tab > Move to Mini Window. The tab shrinks and drops down to the lower right corner by default, staying on top of all other windows (not just Firefox). You can then scroll through the page and follow links as normal in the Mini Window—any links set to open in a new tab (manually or automatically) will launch in the main Firefox view, not the window overlay.

By dragging the title bar around, you can put your Mini Window anywhere you want on screen, and you can change its size by dragging the edges of the window too—so it doesn't necessarily have to be all that "mini." Firefox hides all buttons by default—including forward, backward, and refresh buttons, together with the address bar and buttons for closing the window or putting it back as a normal tab—though they appear when you hover over the Mini Window.

I'm finding the feature most useful when writing up articles where I need to refer to something else already on the web (so almost all of them), but there are a host of ways this can be used. It's particularly handy on a small laptop, where you don't necessarily have the screen space to put two tabs side by side, or when running a lower-priority window (like checking a social media site while reading an important document). No doubt, Firefox will continue to refine and improve the feature as it makes its way to the stable version of the browser, and it feels like something other browsers (looking at you, Google Chrome) might adopt as well in the near future. Eventually, using Mini Windows might become as common as opening up a long row of tabs.

For Talarico and Hinojosa, Whataburger Is a Perfectly Texas Place to Celebrate

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Like so many Texans on so many nights, Senator-elect James Talarico and Governor-elect Gina Hinojosa ended their evening at Whataburger.

Late Tuesday night, after Talarico flipped a Senate seat for the Democrats and Hinojosa was elected the first Democratic governor of the state in more than three decades, the two sat down at an Austin-area location of Whataburger, the iconic regional fast food chain, to clink plastic orange-and-white-striped cups.

It was a fitting setting for the pair, who played critical roles in the blue wave that swept the country in the midterm elections on Tuesday night. Talarico, who defeated scandal-plagued Republican Ken Paxton, went on the offensive against billionaires, prevailing with a positive, populist message against a party funded by Elon Musk and other ultrawealthy donors.

Hinojosa, the first Latina to be elected governor for Texas, targeted corruption and data centers in her race against Republican incumbent Greg Abbott. Both Talarico and Hinojosa will be the first Democrats in more than 30 years to serve Texas in their respective roles.

But even in the midst of a polarizing political moment for the nation, loving Whataburger is bigger than politics in Texas. Since opening its first location in Corpus Christi, Texas, in 1950, the chain, with its signature A-frame buildings, has become a social center for small towns, a place for a budget-friendly meal for families, and a crucial hangout for high schoolers figuring out where else they’ll go that night or stopping for a late-night breakfast before heading home.

Instead of popping Champagne, it’s poignant that Talarico and Hinojosa celebrated with oversize sodas. You can still get a double-burger Whatameal for under $15. These aren't the first Democrats to harness the tangy-sweet appeal of Whataburger's much-beloved Fancy Ketchup. In 2018, the campaign for senate-hopeful Beto O'Rourke shared video of him skateboarding outside a Whataburger before going inside to eat.

Today’s prices are squarely in line with the affordability politics that resonated with voters this cycle. And Talarico and Hinojosa’s Whataburger cheers was a fitting end to a campaign that wove food and politics together. While casting himself as a David against Paxton’s Goliath, Talarico absorbed attacks that claimed he was vegan. While the Senator-elect had urged residents to cut down on beef consumption, he denied the dietary restriction, countering by posting pictures of himself chowing down on barbecue.

Paxton still tried to label his opponent as “Tofu Talarico,” and Trump imagined a scenario where Talarico excuses himself to vomit after eating meat. The barbs were not-so-subtle assaults on Talarico’s manhood, the latest manifestation of the link between meat and masculinity. But there was Talarico on election night, sitting in the house of beef patties, Whatachick’n strips, and bacon-and-egg taquitos.

After a 2019 acquisition by a Chicago-based investment firm, Whataburger has faced allegations of diminishing quality and a nascent rival in Austin’s P. Terry’s Burger Stand. And still, aside from a Dairy Queen or A&W after a high school football game, there may be nowhere in Texas that feels as Texas as a Whataburger when you’re not quite ready to call it a night.

One of the defining features of Whataburger has always been its friendliness to customizing your meal—made to order. Want jalapeños and mustard on your burger, no mayo? Go right ahead, sweetie.

On Tuesday night, as part of national rebuke to President Donald Trump’s party, Texas made its choice clear.

In Mexico City, gorditas are a blank canvas for creative fillings such as stringy quesillo, spiced chorizo, or even a whole fried egg.
